> 1: # SDD — System Design Document
> 2: 
> 3: **Project:** canvas-design  
> 4: **Version:** v0.2 | **Date:** 2026-05-20  
> 5: **BoK Applied:** SWEBOK v4 (Software Design) + CISSP CBK 2024 (Software Development Security)  
> 6: **Status:** Draft — realigned to the open-design sidecar architecture
> 7: 
> 8: ---
> 9: 
> 10: ## 1. Architecture Overview
> 11: 
> 12: `nexu-io/open-design` was **materially verified** on 2026-05-20 (read of `package.json`,
> 13: `CONTEXT.md`, `AGENTS.md`): it is `open-design` v0.7.0, **Apache-2.0**, a standalone
> 14: **local-first design product** — a pnpm monorepo with `apps/web` (Next.js 16), `apps/daemon`
> 15: (the privileged daemon + `od` CLI), `apps/desktop` (Electron), and its own SQLite store
> 16: (`.od/app.sqlite`). It is explicitly **designed to be driven by external agents through the
> 17: `od` CLI**. Canvas Design therefore integrates open-design as a **sovereign sidecar**, not
> 18: as embedded code.
> 19: 
> 20: ```
> 21: ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
> 22: │  MCORCH APP  (Vite · React 18 · bun — login.mcorch.com)            │
> 23: │  Presentation:  src/pages/CanvasDesignPage.tsx                     │
> 24: │    src/components/canvas-design/{DesignGallery, JobStatusPanel,    │
> 25: │      OpenDesignFrame, AssetLibraryPanel, DesignReviewView}.tsx     │
> 26: │  Logic:  src/hooks/{useCanvasDesign, useDesignJobs, useDesignReview}│
> 27: └────────
> <truncated 16929 bytes>
> rged when generation is BYOK? | Charge as platform-usage metering | Free for BYOK | **A** — coins meter platform usage (bridge, daemon, storage, embedding); consistent with the hybrid model | Sovereign |
> 295: | OTD-003 | Per-user credential isolation into `od` | One shared `media-config.json` | Per-user `OD_MEDIA_CONFIG_DIR` mounted per job | **B** — honors the API Tenancy Model; confirm `od` respects per-invocation `OD_MEDIA_CONFIG_DIR` | Engineering |
> 296: | OTD-004 | Review token model | HMAC-signed token (needs a vault secret) | Random 256-bit token in `design_reviews` | **B** — random DB token with expiry; no new secret | Engineering |
> 297: | OTD-005 | Surfacing open-design's editor in MCORCH | New browser tab | nginx-reverse-proxied iframe (`OpenDesignFrame`) | **B** — internal nginx route + iframe keeps the user inside `/dashboard/canvas-design` | Engineering |
> 298: | OTD-006 | How much open-design metadata to mirror into the mesh | Full artifact copy | IDs + summary + render URL + kind (reference node) | **B** — open-design stays system-of-record; mesh holds a searchable reference | Engineering |
> 299: | OTD-007 | Build/runtime isolation — open-design (pnpm + Node 24) vs MCORCH (bun) | Shared toolchain | Dedicated Node 24 + pnpm scoped to `canvas-design/open-design/`; container build | **B** — isolated toolchain; `pnpm` never runs at the MCORCH repo root | Sovereign |

> 10: ## 1. Scope Statement
> 11: 
> 12: This document specifies the functional and non-functional requirements for `canvas-design`.
> 13: It is derived from PRD [`03-prd.md`](03-prd.md) and feeds directly into SDD [`05-sdd.md`](05-sdd.md).
> 14: 
> 15: > **Architecture note (v0.2):** `nexu-io/open-design` is a **standalone local-first
> 16: > product**, not an embeddable engine. Canvas Design integrates it as a **sovereign
> 17: > sidecar**: open-design runs as its own service; MCORCH drives it through the `od` CLI
> 18: > via a host-side `design-bridge` worker and a Supabase `design_jobs` queue.
> 19: 
> 20: **In scope:** the `/dashboard/canvas-design` MCORCH module — design Project gallery and
> 21: launcher, AI job orchestration (generate / refine / export / asset) through the `od`
> 22: bridge, mirroring of open-design artifacts into the Knowledge Mesh as reference nodes,
> 23: MCORCH brand as an open-design design-system, semantic search, and the B2B2C scoped
> 24: review/approval flow.
> 25: 
> 26: **Out of scope:** re-implementing open-design's editor, modifying open-design core,
> 27: real-time multiplayer (PR-021), reviewer-seat billing (PR-022), the template gallery
> 28: (PR-023), and any change to Canvas Studio (`/dashboard/canvas`).
> 29:

> 10: ## 1. Conceptual Data Model
> 11: 
> 12: ```
> 13: [auth.users] ──(1:N)──▶ [design_jobs] ──(1:0..N)──▶ [design_artifact_refs]
> 14:                                                           │ (1:1)
> 15:                                                           ▼
> 16:                                                    [mcorch_nodes: design_artifact]
> 17:                               [design_artifact_refs] ──(1:0..N)──▶ [design_reviews]
> 18: 
> 19:   ┌───────────────────────── EXTERNAL system-of-record ─────────────────────────┐
> 20:   │ open-design `.od/app.sqlite` (per-user OD_DATA_DIR)                          │
> 21:   │   [od Project] ──(1:N)──▶ [od Artifact / HyperFrame]                         │
> 22:   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
> 23:         ▲ mirrored by reference (od_project_id, od_artifact_id)
> 24:         └── design_artifact_refs
> 25: ```
> 26: 
> 27: **Entities (MCORCH-owned, Supabase):**
> 28: - **design_jobs:** an async unit of work (`generate` / `refine` / `export` / `asset`)
> <truncated 12238 bytes>
> ,`od_artifact_id`) | UNIQUE — no duplicate mirror | "Este artefato já está espelhado na mesh." |
> 249: | `design_reviews` | `reviewer_email` | RFC-5322 email format | "Informe um e-mail de revisor válido." |
> 250: | `design_reviews` | `token_expires_at` | Must be in the future on creation | "A validade do link de revisão é inválida." |
> 251: 
> 252: ---
> 253: 
> 254: ## 7. Retention & Archival Policy
> 255: 
> 256: | Data | Retention Period | Archival Strategy | Deletion Trigger |
> 257: |------|-----------------|-------------------|-----------------|
> 258: | `design_jobs` rows | 90 days | None — transient queue records | Cron purge (`status` terminal + age > 90d) |
> 259: | `design_artifact_refs` rows | Indefinite | None | `ON DELETE CASCADE` from `auth.users`; or user erasure |
> 260: | `design_reviews` rows | Indefinite (approval audit trail) | None | `ON DELETE CASCADE` from owner or artifact |
> 261: | `design-assets` bucket objects | Indefinite | None | With the parent `design_artifact_ref` |
> 262: | `mcorch_nodes` `design_artifact` | Indefinite | Backfill via `embed-mcorch-node` | With the user (mesh `user_id` scope) |
> 263: | open-design `OD_DATA_DIR` (per user) | Indefinite | open-design's own export | **Erasure must purge it** — see below |
> 264: 
> 265: **LGPD / GDPR erasure cascade.** A subject-erasure or account deletion request must:
> 266: 1. `DELETE FROM auth.users` → cascades `design_jobs`, `design_artifact_refs`, `design_reviews`.
> 267: 2. Delete the user's `mcorch_nodes` `design_artifact` rows (mesh `user_id` scope).
> 268: 3. Purge `design-assets` objects owned by the user.
> 269: 4. **Purge the user's open-design `OD_DATA_DIR`** — the existing `delete-account` flow does
> 270:    not yet know about open-design and **must be extended** (tracked in `08-quality-metrics.md`
> 271:    FMEA). Without step 4, design data survives erasure → LGPD/GDPR violation.
> 272:

> 1: # Process & Automation Blueprint
> 2: 
> 3: **Project:** canvas-design  
> 4: **Version:** v0.1 | **Date:** 2026-05-20

> 9: 
> 10: ## 1. Process Inventory
> 11: 
> 12: | Process ID | Name | Type | Trigger | Owner | SLA |
> 13: |------------|------|------|---------|-------|-----|
> 14: | PROC-001 | Design Job Lifecycle | event-driven | User runs generate / refine / export / asset | System | ≤ 60s (AI) / ≤ 30s (export) |
> 15: | PROC-002 | Artifact Mirroring to Mesh | automated | A `design_jobs` row reaches `complete` | System | ≤ 5s after job completion |

> 152: ## 6. SLA Definitions

> 154: | Process | Step | Expected Duration | Timeout Threshold | Alert if exceeded |
> 155: |---------|------|------------------|-------------------|-------------------|
> 156: | PROC-001 | `design-job-enqueue` response | < 800ms p95 | 5s | Yes → `infra_health_logs` |

> 169: | `canvas-design-jobs-purge` | `0 4 * * *` | Delete terminal `design_jobs` rows older than 90 days (retention policy, `06-data-model.md` §7) | Age-bounded delete is idempotent | `infra_health_logs` on failure |
> 170: | (watchdog extension) | `*/5 * * * *` | `watchdog-mcorch.sh` adds a health-check of `mcorch-design-daemon` + the `design-bridge` worker | Existing watchdog cron — one added check | `infra_health_logs: canvas_design_daemon_down` |

> Usage:
>   od [--port <n>] [--host <addr>] [--no-open]
>       Start the local daemon and open the web UI.
> 
>   od tools live-artifacts <create|list|update|refresh> [options]
>       Manage live artifacts through daemon wrapper commands.
> 
>   od tools connectors <list|execute> [options]
>       Discover and execute configured connectors.
> 
>   od mcp live-artifacts
>       Start the MCP server exposing live-artifact and connector tools.
> 
>   od research search --query <text> [--max-sources 5] [--daemon-url <url>]
>       Run agent-callable Tavily research through the local daemon.
> 
>   "$OD_NODE_BIN" "$OD_BIN" tools ...
>       Recommended agent-runtime form; avoids relying on user PATH for od or node.
> 
>   od media generate --surface <image|video|audio> --model <id> [opts]
>       Generate a media artifact and write it into the active project.
>       Designed to be invoked by a code agent - picks up OD_DAEMON_URL
>       and OD_PROJECT_ID from the env that the daemon injected on spawn.
> 
>   od mcp [--daemon-url <url>]
>       Run a stdio MCP server that proxies read-only tool calls to a
>       running Open Design daemon. Wire it into a coding agent
>       (Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code, Zed, Windsurf) in another repo
>       to pull files from a local Open Design project without
>       exporting a zip.
> 
> Options:
>   --port <n>       Port to listen on (default: 7456, env: OD_PORT).
>   --host <addr>    Interface address to bind to (default: 127.0.0.1, env: OD_BIND_HOST).
>                    Set to a specific IP (e.g. a Tailscale address) to restrict access
>                    to that interface only.
>   --no-open        Do not open the browser after start.
> 
> What the daemon does:
>   * scans PATH for installed code-agent CLIs (claude, codex, devin, gemini, opencode, cursor-agent, ...)
>   * serves the chat UI at http://<host>:<port>
>   * proxies messages (text + images) to the selected agent via child-process spawn
>   * exposes /api/projects/:id/media/generate — the unified image/video/audio
>      dispatcher that the agent calls via `od media generate`.
> Terminal ID: 151754
> 
> ```

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Discovering Interaction Method**

I've zeroed in on the `POST /api/projects/:id/media/generate` endpoint. I realized the open-design daemon's running and therefore accessible via HTTP on port 7456. Now, I recall `design-bridge` is Sovereign-side, which simplifies things. The bridge can directly call the REST API.


**Refining Execution Strategy**

I've realized the optimal method is for the bridge to invoke `docker exec -i open-design node apps/daemon/dist/cli.js ...` within the container. This approach aligns perfectly with the daemon's architecture and offers robustness. The bridge can write files to `/app/.od` within the container and use `docker cp` to retrieve them. Alternatively, it might use a shared volume to simplify file access between the host and container. I'm going to check if the volume `open-design_open_design_data` is mounted.

</details>

> 7:  *   SUPABASE_URL
> 8:  *   SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY
> 9:  *   SUPABASE_FUNCTIONS_URL  (optional — defaults to SUPABASE_URL/functions/v1)
> 10:  */
> 11: 
> 12: import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'fs';
> 13: import { join, dirname } from 'path';
> 14: import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
> 15: 
> 16: // Load .env
> 17: const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
> 18: const envPath = join(__dirname, '../.env');
> 19: if (existsSync(envPath)) {
> 20:   const raw = readFileSync(envPath, 'utf-8');
> 21:   for (const line of raw.split('\n')) {
> 22:     const m = line.match(/^([^#=\s][^=]*)=(.*)$/);
> 23:     if (m) process.env[m[1].trim()] = m[2].trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, '');
> 24:   }
> 25: }
> 26: 
> 27: const SUPABASE_URL = process.env.SUPABASE_URL;
> 28: const SERVICE_KEY = process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY;
> 29: const FUNCTIONS_URL = process.env.SUPABASE_FUNCTIONS_URL ?? `${SUPABASE_URL}/functions/v1`;
> 30: 
> 31: if (!SUPABASE_URL || !SERVICE_KEY) {
> 32:   console.error('❌ Missing SUPABASE_URL or SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY in .env');
> 33:   process.exit(1);
> 34: }
> 35: 
> 36: const headers = {
> 37:   apikey: SERVICE_KEY,
> 38:   Authorization: `Bearer ${SERVICE_KEY}`,
> 39:   'Content-Type': 'application/json',
> 40: };
> 41: 
> 42: // Fetch nodes with null embedding (paginated)
> 43: async function fetchNullEmbeddingNodes(): Promise<Array<{ id: string; name: string }>> {
> 44:   let all: Array<{ id: string; name: string }> = [];
> 45:   let page = 0;
> 46:   const pageSize = 1000;
> 47:   while (true) {
> 48:     const from = page * pageSize;
> 49:     const to = (page + 1) * pageSize - 1;
> 50:     const res = await fetch(
> 51:       `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes?embedding=is.null&select=id,name&offset=${from}&limit=${pageSize}`,
> 52:       { headers: { ...headers, Range: `${from}-${to}`, 'Range-Unit': 'items' } },
> 53:     );
> 54:     if (!res.ok) {
> 55:       console.error('Failed to fetch nodes:', await res.text());
> 56:       process.exit(1);
> 57:     }
> 58:     const data: Array<{ id: string; name: string }> = await res.json();
> 59:     all = all.concat(data);
> 60:     if (data.length < pageSize) break;
> 61:     page++;
> 62:   }
> 63:   return all;
> 64: }
> 65: 
> 66: const nodes = await fetchNullEmbeddingNodes();
> 67: console.log(`🔍 Found ${nodes.length} nodes without embeddings.`);
> 68: 
> 69: if (nodes.length === 0) {
> 70:   console.log('✅ All nodes already have embeddings.');
> 71:   process.exit(0);
> 72: }
> 73: 
> 74: let ok = 0;
> 75: let fail = 0;
> 76: 
> 77: for (const node of nodes) {
> 78:   const embedRes = await fetch(`${FUNCTIONS_URL}/embed-mcorch-node`, {
> 79:     method: 'POST',
> 80:     headers,
> 81:     body: JSON.stringify({ node_id: node.id }),
> 82:   });
> 83: 
> 84:   if (embedRes.ok) {
> 85:     const result = await embedRes.json();
> 86:     process.stdout.write(`\r  ✅ ${ok + 1}/${nodes.length} embedded — last: ${node.name.slice(0, 30)}`);
> 87:     ok++;
> 88:   } else {
> 89:     const err = await embedRes.text();
> 90:     console.error(`\n  ✗ ${node.name} (${node.id.slice(0, 8)}) — ${err.slice(0, 120)}`);
> 91:     fail++;
> 92:   }
> 93: 
> 94:   // Rate-limit: ~2 req/s to avoid OpenRouter quota
> 95:   await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 500));
> 96: }
> 97: 
> 98: console.log(`\n\n🏁 Done: ${ok} embedded · ${fail} failed`);
> 99: if (fail > 0) process.exit(1);
> 100: 
> The above content shows the entire, complete file contents of the requested file.
> 
> ```

**Considering the Structure**

Okay, I'm finding this setup to load `.env` and set headers neat for interacting with the REST API. However, I need to focus on the `design-bridge.ts` worker. Specifically, it needs to query the `design_jobs` table. The goal is to filter where `status` is 'queued', but I'm thinking about how to actually fetch from it.


**Refining the Atomic Update**

I've been drilling down on atomic updates for the worker. The crucial PostgREST trick is confirmed: a `PATCH` request with a `status=queued` filter will only succeed if the job is actually queued. This prevents race conditions. Now, the `Prefer: return=representation` header will let me see the updated job immediately. I'm ready to move into designing the actual worker loop now.


**Detailing the Job Logic**

I'm now diving into the `processJob(job)` function. The core challenge is the switch based on `job.kind`. For 'generate', I've spotted a critical step: the `od_project_id` check. If it's missing, I'll need to create a project in Open Design via `/api/projects`. I need to figure out the best approach to generate a project ID, as well as name. The FRD stipulates per-user data directories, which should be helpful.


**Clarifying the Daemon**

I've clarified the Open Design daemon's operation. It runs within the container and uses a single SQLite database at `/app/.od/app.sqlite`. This means a single instance handles all projects. To create a project, the API call should be `POST http://127.0.0.1:7456/api/projects` with a payload including the project ID and name. I am now thinking of how the `od` CLI can be executed inside the container for generating media. I'm checking the `od media generate` syntax.

> 2: 
> 3: Este plano descreve as etapas técnicas para implementar a primeira etapa dos próximos passos: **Integrar e validar o Canvas Design (nexu-io/open-design)**, seguindo a especificação descrita na suíte de documentação BoK `canvas-design` e a diretiva de sidecar do Sovereign.
> 4: 
> 5: ## User Review Required
> 6: 
> 7: Documentamos abaixo as decisões tomadas para aprovação do Sovereign:
> 8: 
> 9: > [!IMPORTANT]
> 10: > **Dedução de mcoCoins:** Cada operação de geração de mídia consumirá saldo da carteira do usuário de forma atômica no Supabase:
> 11: > - `generate` (criar design): 6 mcoCoins
> 12: > - `refine` (refinar design existente): 3 mcoCoins
> 13: > - `asset` (gerar asset secundário): 2 mcoCoins
> 14: > - `export` (exportar formato PNG/SVG/PDF): 0 mcoCoins
> 15: >
> 16: > A cobrança é feita via RPC server-side no enfileiramento do job. Caso o saldo seja insuficiente, a API retornará `HTTP 402` (Payment Required) e o job não será criado.
> 17: 
> 18: > [!TIP]
> 19: > **Execução Headless da Engine:** O daemon do `open-design` rodará em container Docker isolado (`open-design`), expondo a porta `7456` na rede interna (já inicializado e configurado com `OD_MEDIA_ALLOW_STUBS: "1"` para habilitar stubs). O `design-bridge` rodará no host via Bun/Node, subscrevendo à fila `design_jobs` e disparando chamadas CLI via `docker exec -i open-design node apps/daemon/dist/cli.js`.

> 2: /**
> 3:  * Backfill embeddings for all mcorch_nodes that have embedding = NULL.
> 4:  * Usage: bun run scripts/backfill-embeddings.ts
> 5:  *
> 6:  * Requires .env with:
> 7:  *   SUPABASE_URL
> 8:  *   SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY
> 9:  *   SUPABASE_FUNCTIONS_URL  (optional — defaults to SUPABASE_URL/functions/v1)

> 11: 
> 12: import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'fs';
> 13: import { join, dirname } from 'path';
> 14: import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
> 15: 
> 16: // Load .env
> 17: const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
> 18: const envPath = join(__dirname, '../.env');
> 19: if (existsSync(envPath)) {
> 20:   const raw = readFileSync(envPath, 'utf-8');
> 21:   for (const line of raw.split('\n')) {
> 22:     const m = line.match(/^([^#=\s][^=]*)=(.*)$/);
> 23:     if (m) process.env[m[1].trim()] = m[2].trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, '');
> 24:   }
> 25: }
> 26: 
> 27: const SUPABASE_URL = process.env.SUPABASE_URL;
> 28: const SERVICE_KEY = process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY;
> 29: const FUNCTIONS_URL = process.env.SUPABASE_FUNCTIONS_URL ?? `${SUPABASE_URL}/functions/v1`;
> 30: 
> 31: if (!SUPABASE_URL || !SERVICE_KEY) {
> 32:   console.error('❌ Missing SUPABASE_URL or SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY in .env');
> 33:   process.exit(1);
> 34: }
> 35: 
> 36: const headers = {
> 37:   apikey: SERVICE_KEY,
> 38:   Authorization: `Bearer ${SERVICE_KEY}`,
> 39:   'Content-Type': 'application/json',
> 40: };
> 41: 
> 42: // Fetch nodes with null embedding (paginated)
> 43: async function fetchNullEmbeddingNodes(): Promise<Array<{ id: string; name: string }>> {
> 44:   let all: Array<{ id: string; name: string }> = [];
> 45:   let page = 0;
> 46:   const pageSize = 1000;
> 47:   while (true) {
> 48:     const from = page * pageSize;
> 49:     const to = (page + 1) * pageSize - 1;
> 50:     const res = await fetch(
> 51:       `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes?embedding=is.null&select=id,name&offset=${from}&limit=${pageSize}`,
> 52:       { headers: { ...headers, Range: `${from}-${to}`, 'Range-Unit': 'items' } },
> 53:     );
> 54:     if (!res.ok) {
> 55:       console.error('Failed to fetch nodes:', await res.text());
> 56:       process.exit(1);
> 57:     }
> 58:     const data: Array<{ id: string; name: string }> = await res.json();
> 59:     all = all.concat(data);
> 60:     if (data.length < pageSize) break;
> 61:     page++;
> 62:   }
> 63:   return all;
> 64: }
> 65: 
> 66: const nodes = await fetchNullEmbeddingNodes();
> 67: console.log(`🔍 Found ${nodes.length} nodes without embeddings.`);
> 68: 
> 69: if (nodes.length === 0) {
> 70:   console.log('✅ All nodes already have embeddings.');
> 71:   process.exit(0);
> 72: }
> 73: 
> 74: let ok = 0;
> 75: let fail = 0;
> 76: 
> 77: for (const node of nodes) {
> 78:   const embedRes = await fetch(`${FUNCTIONS_URL}/embed-mcorch-node`, {
> 79:     method: 'POST',
> 80:     headers,
> 81:     body: JSON.stringify({ node_id: node.id }),
> 82:   });
> 83: 
> 84:   if (embedRes.ok) {
> 85:     const result = await embedRes.json();
> 86:     process.stdout.write(`\r  ✅ ${ok + 1}/${nodes.length} embedded — last: ${node.name.slice(0, 30)}`);
> 87:     ok++;
> 88:   } else {
> 89:     const err = await embedRes.text();
> 90:     console.error(`\n  ✗ ${node.name} (${node.id.slice(0, 8)}) — ${err.slice(0, 120)}`);
> 91:     fail++;
> 92:   }
> 93: 
> 94:   // Rate-limit: ~2 req/s to avoid OpenRouter quota
> 95:   await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 500));
> 96: }
> 97: 
> 98: console.log(`\n\n🏁 Done: ${ok} embedded · ${fail} failed`);
> 99: if (fail > 0) process.exit(1);
> 100:

> 12:  * Output structure:
> 13:  *   memory/mcorch-export/
> 14:  *     tier1/<node_type>/<id>.md
> 15:  *     tier2/<node_type>/<id>.md
> 16:  *     _meta/edges.jsonl          (one edge per line)
> 17:  *     _meta/manifest.json        (counts + timestamp + filters)
> 18:  *
> 19:  * Usage:
> 20:  *   bun run scripts/bridge-mesh-to-openclaw.ts                 # full export
> 21:  *   bun run scripts/bridge-mesh-to-openclaw.ts --dry-run       # count + classify only
> 22:  *   bun run scripts/bridge-mesh-to-openclaw.ts --since="24 hours ago"  # incremental (created_at filter)
> 23:  *   bun run scripts/bridge-mesh-to-openclaw.ts --tier=1        # only Tier 1
> 24:  *
> 25:  * Requires .env with SUPABASE_URL + SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY.

> 				Usage:
>   od [--port <n>] [--host <addr>] [--no-open]
>       Start the local daemon and open the web UI.
> 
>   od tools live-artifacts <create|list|update|refresh> [options]
>       Manage live artifacts through daemon wrapper commands.
> 
>   od tools connectors <list|execute> [options]
>       Discover and execute configured connectors.
> 
>   od mcp live-artifacts
>       Start the MCP server exposing live-artifact and connector tools.
> 
>   od research search --query <text> [--max-sources 5] [--daemon-url <url>]
>       Run agent-callable Tavily research through the local daemon.
> 
>   "$OD_NODE_BIN" "$OD_BIN" tools ...
>       Recommended agent-runtime form; avoids relying on user PATH for od or node.
> 
>   od media generate --surface <image|video|audio> --model <id> [opts]
>       Generate a media artifact and write it into the active project.
>       Designed to be invoked by a code agent - picks up OD_DAEMON_URL
>       and OD_PROJECT_ID from the env that the daemon injected on spawn.
> 
>   od mcp [--daemon-url <url>]
>       Run a stdio MCP server that proxies read-only tool calls to a
>       running Open Design daemon. Wire it into a coding agent
>       (Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code, Zed, Windsurf) in another repo
>       to pull files from a local Open Design project without
>       exporting a zip.
> 
> Options:
>   --port <n>       Port to listen on (default: 7456, env: OD_PORT).
>   --host <addr>    Interface address to bind to (default: 127.0.0.1, env: OD_BIND_HOST).
>                    Set to a specific IP (e.g. a Tailscale address) to restrict access
>                    to that interface only.
>   --no-open        Do not open the browser after start.
> 
> What the daemon does:
>   * scans PATH for installed code-agent CLIs (claude, codex, devin, gemini, opencode, cursor-agent, ...)
>   * serves the chat UI at http://<host>:<port>
>   * proxies messages (text + images) to the selected agent via child-process spawn
>   * exposes /api/projects/:id/media/generate — the unified image/video/audio
>      dispatcher that the agent calls via `od media generate`.
> Terminal ID: 151754
> 
> ```

> 13: `CONTEXT.md`, `AGENTS.md`): it is `open-design` v0.7.0, **Apache-2.0**, a standalone
> 14: **local-first design product** — a pnpm monorepo with `apps/web` (Next.js 16), `apps/daemon`
> 15: (the privileged daemon + `od` CLI), `apps/desktop` (Electron), and its own SQLite store
> 16: (`.od/app.sqlite`). It is explicitly **designed to be driven by external agents through the
> 17: `od` CLI**. Canvas Design therefore integrates open-design as a **sovereign sidecar**, not
> 18: as embedded code.

> 181: ## 5. Security Design
> 182: 
> 183: ### 5.1 Threat Model (STRIDE)
> 184: 
> 185: | Component | Threat | Type | Severity | Mitigation |
> 186: |-----------|--------|------|----------|------------|
> 187: | `design-job-enqueue` | Unauthenticated invocation | Spoofing | High | JWT validation at entry, before any logic |
> 188: | `design_jobs` / `_artifact_refs` / `_reviews` | Cross-tenant data read | Information Disclosure | Critical | RLS `user_id = auth.uid()` default-deny on every table |
> 189: | mcoCoins deduction | Client-side bypass / double charge | Tampering | Critical | Server-side `deduct_mco_coins` RPC; deduction before the job row insert |
> 190: | `mcorch-design-daemon` (`od`) | Public access to the design daemon | Spoofing / DoS | High | Daemon bound to the internal Docker network only — never a public port |
> 191: | Per-user AI credentials | One tenant's key used for another's job | Information Disclosure / Tampering | Critical | Bridge runs `od` with a per-user `OD_MEDIA_CONFIG_DIR`; credentials never shared across jobs |
> 192: | `design-bridge` | Compromised worker reaches all tenants' data | Elevation of Privilege | High | Worker holds the service role — runs only on the Sovereign host, no inbound ports, least-privilege filesystem |
> 193: | `design-review-access` | Forged or guessed review token | Spoofing | High | 256-bit random token, unique-indexed, time-limited, revocable; read + decision only |
> 194: | `design-assets` bucket | A user reads another tenant's render | Information Disclosure | High | Private bucket; short-lived signed URLs scoped to the owner |
> 195:

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Investigating Command Fallback**

I've just observed that invoking the CLI without a specified subcommand defaults to starting the daemon, resulting in an "EADDRINUSE" error. Now, I'm testing the `tools` and `list` subcommands to see if they behave differently.


**Clarifying CLI Functionality**

I've checked the CLI's top-level help and noticed that the `project export` subcommand is missing, contradicting a draft document. The actual CLI version I have doesn't have it. Instead, the `export` operation seems to be handled by copying existing files. `Refine` and `asset` operations appear to be equivalent to running `od media generate --surface image --model <model> --prompt "<prompt>"` or similar, potentially referencing an existing image. I found that `od media generate` has an `--image` option.


**Connecting Artifacts and Generation**

I've realized the `--image` flag in `media generate` is the key! It accepts a project-relative path. If a user provides an `od_artifact_id`, I can retrieve the filename from the database, likely matching the `od_artifact_id`. That artifact filename can then be passed to `--image`. The `design_artifact_refs` table links `od_project_id`, `od_artifact_id`, and `render_url`. Furthermore, the CLI returns the filename in `file.name`, verifying `od_artifact_id` is the filename itself!


**Defining Design Bridge Steps**

Now, I'm sketching out the structure for `scripts/design-bridge.ts`. The primary focus is on managing queued design jobs. I'll need to fetch a job, then claim it using a REST API `PATCH` request for atomic updates. Based on the job parameters, I'll then construct the appropriate Docker command. Handling `generate`, `asset`, and `refine` actions requires specific logic to handle the surface, model, prompt, aspect, and, crucially, the `--image` flag for artifact references, along with injecting project ID via env vars for clean container execution.

</details>

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Locating Relevant Files**

I've pinpointed the compiled server and CLI code, residing in `/app/apps/daemon/dist`. Specifically, I've noted `cli.js`, `server.js`, and `tools-live-artifacts-cli.js`. Now, I'm shifting focus to understanding how `media generate` functions within this structure.


**Analyzing Media Generation**

I've located `/app/apps/daemon/dist/src/media.js` and confirmed its role in media processing, alongside its type definition. I'm now designing `scripts/design-bridge.ts`, which I plan to implement as a continuous Bun daemon with a `while(true)` loop and a 2000ms sleep. It will initialize by loading `.env` variables and connect to Supabase, via `@supabase/supabase-js`, which imports fine. I'm using `fetch`, for standard headers. The script will query the `design_jobs` table for queued items, looping with standard Node `fetch` headers.


**Implementing Docker Execution**

I'm now detailing the job execution logic within my daemon. Specifically, I'm focusing on atomic job claiming using Supabase updates to prevent race conditions. If a job is claimed, I will begin the docker execution phase. This involves writing a `runDockerCommand` function that uses Node's `child_process.spawn` for asynchronous, non-blocking execution. The function will also handle capturing stdout and stderr.


**Refining Docker Command**

I've been refining the Docker execution command string. I'm now constructing the arguments array with the `--model` flag, defaulting to 'dall-e-3'. I'm also adding a conditional `--aspect` flag and checking for `refine` and `asset` job kinds and then applying an `--image` parameter if `od_artifact_id` is present. Additionally, I've had a breakthrough! I realized that per-user credential isolation can be easily achieved by mounting the `OD_MEDIA_CONFIG_DIR`. {
  "CodeContent": "\"import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';\\nimport { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';\\nimport { useAuth } from '@/hooks/useAuth';\\n\\nexport interface DesignArtifactRef {\\n  id: string;\\n  user_id: string;\\n  project_id: string | null;\\n  od_project_id: string;\\n  od_artifact_id: string;\\n  kind: 'hyperframe' | 'asset';\\n  title: string;\\n  summary: string | null;\\n  render_url: string | null;\\n  mesh_node_id: string | null;\\n  source_job_id: string | null;\\n  created_at: string;\\n  updated_at: string;\\n}\\n\\nexport function useCanvasDesign(projectId?: string) {\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\n\\n  // Query to fetch mirrored design artifact references\\n  const { data: artifacts, isLoading, error, refetch } = useQuery({\\n    queryKey: ['design-refs', user?.id, projectId],\\n    queryFn: async () => {\\n      let query = supabase\\n        .from('design_artifact_refs')\\n        .select('*')\\n        .eq('user_id', user!.id)\\n        .order('created_at', { ascending: false });\\n\\n      if (projectId) {\\n        // Here project_id corresponds to campaigns(id)\\n        query = query.eq('project_id', projectId);\\n      }\\n\\n      const { data, error } = await query;\\n      if (error) throw error;\\n      return data as DesignArtifactRef[];\\n    },\\n    enabled: !!user?.id,\\n  });\\n\\n  // Fetch open-design projects from the daemon\\n  const { data: odProjects, isLoading: isODProjectsLoading } = useQuery({\\n    queryKey: ['design-projects'],\\n    queryFn: async () => {\\n      const res = await fetch('http://127.0.0.1:7456/api/projects');\\n      if (!res.ok) throw new Error('Failed to fetch open-design projects');\\n      const data = await res.json();\\n      return data.projects || [];\\n    },\\n  });\\n\\n  return {\\n    artifacts,\\n    isLoading: isLoading || isODProjectsLoading,\\n    error,\\n    odProjects,\\n    refetch,\\n  };\\n}\\n\"",
  "Description": "\"Create useCanvasDesign hook for loading artifacts and open-design projects\"",
